I would also like to stress that this law or the draft law on the Supreme Court cannot be judged separately from the laws already adopted on the National Council of Courts and the system of common courts. There has been a statement in the media space that these rules, jointly understood or considered, constitute a technological sequence - the idea is to bring about a full politicisation of the judiciary in Poland. We, as the National Judicial Council, raised a number of objections to the draft law on the National Judicial Council. The three solutions adopted in this law are, in our view, clearly incompatible with the Constitution in the assessment of the National Courts Council. These are: shortening the term of office of the members of the National Judicial Council - let us add, it is only a matter of judges elected to the National Judicial Council, and this term is four years old, it is quite obvious in the Constitution, we believe that the law of this term cannot be shortened - the division of the National Judicial Council into two assemblies, the assembly of politicians and the assembly of judges - does not have any constitutional basis to do so, because the Constitution provides that the National Judicial Council consists of 25 persons and each member of the National Judicial Council has the same vote in the National Judicial Council as a collegial body, the National Judicial Council adopts resolutions by majority, the entire National Judicial Council and not the assembly; in this assembly of politicians, judges will constitute a minority, which will block any resolution which will be uncomfortable for politicians. This is clearly contrary not only to the constitution, but also to European standards, which provide that judges should be a majority in the National Council of the Court of Justice. Why? Because it is one of the guarantees of the independence of the judiciary. The choice of judges is one of the guarantees of independence of the judiciary. And the third issue, which involves the choice of judges on the board. I will not repeat these arguments which indicate that judges should be elected to the National Judicial Council themselves. This requires an examination of the rules, we have made such an analysis, these analyses are in the justifications of the position of the National Council of Courts, but we have also pointed out that the adoption of such a model, that politicians will point to judges who will be elected to the Council, will lead to a politicisation of the selection of judges to the board. One might wonder when such a political choice might be helpful. It seems that this may happen in a moment, because, as I said, there are provisions in the Act on the National Council of Courts which confer certain powers on the National Council of Courts, probably already the new one. Now I would like to draw attention to a very important issue, although mostly relevant to lawyers, i.e. the formal issue related to the adoption of this project. In fact, there are certain rules of law in the state of law. With regard to legislation of a systemic nature - and with such legislation we are dealing with here, because the Supreme Court Act is certainly the Law on system issues - this type of regulation should pay particular attention to the process of law-making, because this is extremely important. The National Judicial Council Act states that any draft law which concerns the functioning of the judiciary should be assessed by the National Judicial Council. We found out about this project on Friday. As chairman of the National Judicial Council, I called for tomorrow... sorry, for today's extraordinary meeting of the National Judicial Council because there was information that this project was to be recognized tomorrow. [[Scandal and disgrace.]] This delay in project recognition means that the National Judicial Council will not have the opportunity to present its opinion in the manner provided for in the law. I would also like to remind you that infringement of the rules on the adoption of laws can be a basis for considering them as non-constitutional, regardless of what the content of those provisions is, so I would suggest to you Members of this law, the initiators of this law, that they also draw attention to it. Since we did not have the opportunity to draw up this opinion, I would now like to present to you the draft position of the National Judicial Council on this law. The Supreme Court is the first court of the State to supervise the activities of all universal and military courts in the field of judgment. That court therefore has constitutional legitimacy to influence the content of even final decisions issued by the courts. The Supreme Court not only recognises the means of appeal against decisions of general and military courts, but, above all, has the power to adopt resolutions aimed at clarifying the legal provisions of doubt or the application of which has led to divergences in case law, as well as resolutions containing the resolution of legal issues of serious doubt in a specific case which the Court of Justice considers. The implementation of these extremely important competences of the Supreme Court increases the level of uniformity of case law in the various areas of the functioning of the State. The previous authority of the Supreme Court makes the interpretation of the laws by the formations of the courts, although it has no universal force, respected by the representatives of the law and recognised by the courts. The Supreme Court guarantees the democratic and legitimate choice of public authorities. First President of the Supreme Court indicates three judges The Supreme Court of the State Election Commission. Moreover, the Constitution entrusted the Supreme Court with the competence to deal with electoral protests, to declare the validity of elections to the Sejm, the Senate and the President of the Republic of Poland, as well as the validity of the national referendum. On a similar basis, the Supreme Court was granted the power to recognise appeals against the resolutions of the National Council of Courts concerning the submission of applications for the appointment of judges to the President of the Republic of Poland. The Supreme Court therefore ensures that the appointment to exercise legislative, executive and judicial authority is lawful. In addition, the Supreme Court recognises the legal measures relating to the functioning of political parties, thereby affecting the pluralistic nature of the public debate. It is also important that the first President of the Supreme Court of the Republic of Poland is President of the Court of State and as the only person not elected by the Sejm may rule on constitutional liability of the President of the Republic of Poland, members of the government and other key public officials. The Supreme Court, under both the current rules and after the law has entered into force, exercises jurisdiction over disciplinary proceedings against judges, prosecutors, notaries and persons engaged in legal professions such as lawyer, bailiff and legal adviser. The Supreme Court also controls the legality of certain decisions taken by professional authorities. This means that the Supreme Court has powers which directly affect the status and powers of persons who are called upon to exercise justice, to prosecute crimes, to protect citizens from abuse by representatives of power and legal assistance. Given that the Minister of Justice has administrative oversight The Supreme Court has always remained outside the sphere of influence of the Minister. This demonstrates the strong independence of the Supreme Court from political power, so that the Supreme Court judges could carry out their judicial activity in the public interest, based on their advanced legal knowledge. In this way and the apoliticality of the Supreme Court, citizens can rely on the independent and independent court to identify their cases in lower instances. The current rules granted important powers to the President of the Republic of Poland, who by regulation regulated important issues for the operation of the Supreme Court. The project marginalizes the role of the President of the Republic of Poland, and the Minister of Justice takes over the powers of the President of the Republic of Poland, as well as numerous powers which have so far been conferred on the first President of the Supreme Court. These and other tasks carried out by the Supreme Court make it necessary for its status to be specifically protected under the democratic rule of law as a basis for the structure and functioning of the judiciary. And on this subject we have many statements from the representatives of the doctrine. Therefore, the amendments to the rules governing the operation of the Supreme Court should be comprehensively and comprehensively analysed and introduced without haste in order to guarantee the highest standard of legislation. The provisions governing the operation of the Supreme Court must fully respect the constitutional principles of the division of powers, the separation and independence of courts and the independence of judges. As the Constitutional Tribunal has pointed out, the principle of division of power implies a special way of determining the relationship between the judicial authority and the other authorities. In relations between the legislative and executive authorities, different forms of interaction are possible. Areas where the powers of the authorities belonging to the two authorities are intersecting or imposing may also exist. On the other hand, the relationship between the judicial authority and the other authorities must be based on the principle of separation. The independence of the courts and the independence of the judges are a necessary element of the principle of division of power. The principles of separation and independence of the courts assume the separation of the judiciary from the authority of government and parliament officials. In turn, the independence of the judge consists of factors such as impartiality towards the parties involved in the proceedings, independence from out-of-court institutions, independence from judicial authorities, independence from political influences, especially political parties, and the internal independence of the judge. These are also widely accepted views in doctrine, and I will not quote the authors of these statements. The draft law on the Supreme Court abolishes the independence of judges towards out-of-court institutions and, above all, its freedom from political and party influences. In view of the fact that the Minister of Justice is acting as the Attorney General, judges whose fate will be so heavily dependent on the will of the Minister of Justice may lose the quality of impartiality vis-à-vis participants in judicial proceedings. In the opinion of the National Council of Courts, the opinion of the bill is an act of gross violation of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, because it undermines the constitutional guarantees of independence and separation of the Supreme Court and the authority of this body, which since its renewal in free Poland after 1990 has been built by generations of the most outstanding Polish lawyers. Now with regard to the transitional provisions. The proposalor proposes that the day after the date of entry into force of the bill, all judges of the Supreme Court should be retiring by law, with the exception of judges designated by the Minister of Justice. The author of the project suggests that the admissibility of the transfer of the judges of the Supreme Court to rest results from the article. Point (b) of Article 4 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 5 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, according to which, in the event of a change in the system of courts or a change of the boundaries of judicial districts, a judge may be transferred to another court or at rest, leaving him full remuneration. However, the justification of the bill in question proves that the purpose of the changes introduced in the Supreme Court is not to change the system of the courts and, above all, to change its composition and adjust its case law to the expectations of politicians. The obligation to retire provided for in Article. Article 87 (1) of the proposal should therefore be explicitly assessed as a certain repression against judges of the Supreme Court. The National Judicial Council recalls that even in the event of a genuine change in the judicial system, the transfer of the judge to rest is a measure of a definitive nature and must be subject to judicial review in accordance with constitutional and conventional standards for the protection of the rights of the individual. Here, of course, no guarantees of protection of the rights of judges who are to retire are foreseen, there is no procedure for appeal against this decision to retire. The explanatory memorandum states that, after its entry into force, following the proposed creation of new chambers and departments and changes of cognition and procedure, the Supreme Court will in fact become a completely new court, both in a systemic and functional way. In our opinion, the project does not change the system of the Supreme Court. It is impossible to change the system of the Supreme Court without amending the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which results from the fundamental system regulations of the Supreme Court. The Supreme Court will continue to remain the body exercising constitutional oversight over the activities of universal courts and military courts. It will also continue to have the constitutional powers conferred solely on the Supreme Court to recognise electoral protests and to determine the validity of national elections and referendums. Moreover, the court will continue to recognise disciplinary matters of judges and legal practitioners and other cases entrusted to it by other laws. It must be made clear that the change in the number and names of the internal units of the Supreme Court cannot be identified as a change in the system of the Supreme Court. What else is the internal organisation of the court, i.e. the division of the court into chambers or departments, and otherwise the system of courts, i.e. the structure and interrelationship of the courts within the framework of the general, administrative and military courts. The organist uses these concepts in different places. There's a reason in the article. Point (b) of Article 4 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 5 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland on the grounds of the transfer to rest of the judge chose to change the system of the courts, not to change the organisation of the internal court. [[Of course, bravo.]] In particular, the nature of the proposed organisational changes within the Supreme Court is in favour of this position. So far there have been chambers in the Supreme Court corresponding to the internal structure of the general and military judiciary: the Civil Chamber, the Penal Chamber, the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs and the Military Chamber. Such a solution was determined by a transparent division of jurisdiction between the Supreme Court chambers. Currently, the projector proposes that the Supreme Court should have three chambers: the Chamber of Private Law, the Chamber of Public Law and the Disciplinary Chamber. While the designer clearly indicates the property of the latter, for the first two in the justification of the project, it was concluded that this division is a reference to the dichotomy-based division into public law and private law based on the subject-matter of the regulation, the nature of the entities and the method of action of the law, i.e. shaping legal relations and legal situations. However, this wording has nothing to do with the constitutional structure of the judiciary. Public and private law cases are dealt with by different courts, including administrative courts, and in different procedures. In practice, therefore, a change in the internal organisation of the Supreme Court boils down to the creation of a Disciplinary Chamber, which is granted certain powers of the existing Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs. To the rest, the proposals lead to a change in the names of the Civil Chamber to the Chamber of Private Law and the Criminal Chamber and the Military Chamber to the Chamber of Public Law and the division between the three Chambers of the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs. Therefore, this is not even an advanced organisational change, since it is limited in practice to changes in the names of organisational units and the associated relocation of certain categories of cases. It is therefore necessary to reiterate that the proposed law does not change the system of the Supreme Court and does not in any case justify a transfer to the rest of the judges of the Supreme Court appointed to act on the basis of the existing provisions. Nor does it give rise to the transfer of Supreme Court judges to judicial positions at the lower levels of the judiciary. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 87 of this project was granted to the Minister of Justice to designate judges of the Supreme Court who would not be legally transferred to retirement. The designer shall not indicate any criteria to be followed by the Minister of Justice when making that decision. The judge appointed by the Minister to remain active in the judicial service will not be able to appeal against that decision. This solution confers on the Minister of Justice, unknown to the Polish constitutional system the competence to determine the composition of the Supreme Court by the Minister of Justice on his own, on any, discreet and unfettered basis. Such a right is a violation of the constitutional principle of separation between judicial authorities and other authorities. Only the President of the Republic of Poland, acting at the request of the National Judicial Council, may influence the composition of the court by making the appointments of the judges. In the light of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the Minister of Justice has no powers relating to the appointment of judges of the Supreme Court outside the powers which he has as a member of the National Council of Courts. I would like to add here that the Minister does not exercise these powers because he has been to the Council twice, about a year and a half ago. Under the proposed law, the Minister of Justice will be able to shape the composition of the Supreme Court freely, which will deprive all the judges of the Supreme Court of independence and will constitute a government repression for the previous judicial activity. This solution is, to a similar extent, derogatory to those judges of the Supreme Court who are going to retire and have so far held their office with sacrifice, as well as those whom the Minister will leave in active service as chosen. For this reason, they may lose social trust and the quality of political impartiality, which is, let's add, a serious threat. If we are talking about the impartiality of the courts, we are usually thinking about the guarantees of impartiality granted by law, but the doctrine of law makes it clear that as important as the elements of impartiality granted to the law are in the perception of society, whether the judge is considered impartial. If, in the public opinion, the judge is not considered impartial, he will not meet this constitutional condition of an impartial judge. So all the changes that undermine the public's confidence in the impartiality of the court are, in fact, changes in the impartiality of the court. [[Applause]] The National Council of the Court notes that the opinion proposes only the temporary removal of the President of the Republic of Poland of his constitutional competence to appoint the first President of the Supreme Court. This is provided for in Article. Point (c) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 3. The designer wants the task and competence of the first President of the Supreme Court to be performed by the judge appointed by the Minister of Justice in the event of the expected removal from the Supreme Court of the present first President of the Supreme Court. It should be stressed that the office of the first President of the Supreme Court is determined solely by the tasks and duties assigned to him. Therefore, if any officer is entrusted with the tasks and duties of the first President of the Supreme Court, this delegation may only take place under the procedure laid down in Article. Point (c) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 3rd Constitution. The Minister of Justice cannot therefore assume the constitutional competence of the General Assembly of Judges of the Supreme Court to designate candidates for the position of the first President of the Supreme Court or even the more constitutional competence of the President of the Republic of Poland to appoint the first President of the Supreme Court, even for a transitional period. It is worth noting that the projector gives the Minister more power in this provision to appoint the first President of the Supreme Court than the one who has the President of the Republic of Poland. The Minister may entrust these activities to the elected Supreme Court judge, and the President of the Republic of Poland may entrust only one of the candidates designated by the General Assembly of Supreme Court Judges. It is equally important that the appointment of the Minister of Justice may serve as the first President of the Supreme Court for a more indeterminate period of time, since in accordance with Article. 93 § 3 of the proposal, the selection of candidates for the first President may be carried out only after the last vacant position of the Supreme Court judge has been taken. In addition, it should be pointed out that the Constitution of the Republic of Poland includes the appointment of the first President of the Supreme Court in the catalogue of presidential prerogatives, which clearly assumes that government representatives do not participate in this procedure. The submission of executive positions to the Supreme Court also concerns the positions of the Presidents of the Supreme Court in charge of the work of the various chambers. This is because it is only the Minister of Justice who will decide who the President of the Republic of Poland will be able to appoint for the first time as Presidents of the Supreme Court on the basis of the draft law on the Supreme Court currently under review. This competence was granted to the Minister of Justice, although the main text of the project concerns Article. 14 § 2, that meeting of the Judges of the Chamber shall have the right to designate three candidates for the post of President of the Supreme Court. Thus, the Minister of Justice not only eliminates the participation of the Assembly of Judges of the Chamber in the first nomination of the President of the Supreme Court, but also limits the powers of the President of the Republic of Poland, who will have to approve the ministerial candidate. In this respect, too, this solution is in breach of the constitutional prohibition to make the appointment of the Supreme Court President conditional on the approval of a government representative. However, this does not result in the special powers of the Minister of Justice towards the Supreme Court and its judges. In view of the non-constitutional displacement of judges, the drafter proposes a special way of supplementing the Supreme Court with new judges. This procedure will not be open to all those concerned who meet the statutory criteria for appointment but only to persons who will be designated by the Minister of Justice. Only the Minister of Justice will be able to designate the National Judicial Council as candidates for judges. It is not clear how the Minister will choose and select these candidates, which makes this solution not only undermines the independence of future ministerial Supreme Court judges, but is also corrupt. This solution also violates the constitutional right of access to public service under the same conditions, inter alia. by prohibiting the applicant from submitting its own application and excluding the possibility for the candidate to appeal to the Supreme Court from the resolution of the National Judicial Council. Let us add at this point that, under the current procedure of nominating judges, any resolution of the National Court of Justice was subject to appeal to the Supreme Court and that all persons who were candidates for the vacant seat of the Supreme Court, in the event that they disagreed with the resolution of the National Court of Justice, could appeal to the Supreme Court, which guaranteed a judicial route on the assessment of the resolution of the National Court of Justice. I did not find in the justification of the draft arguments which argue that this road is to be excluded and that this is to be part of the democratisation of access to justice. What is equally important, the drafter shall require the Council to consider these applications within 14 days. It should be stressed here that these nominations are likely to be considered by these two separate Council meetings, in accordance with the already amended provisions on the National Courts of Justice. In the light of Art. 94 § 4 of this project, the unsuccessful expiry of this period will be equivalent to the presentation by the National Judicial Council to the President of the Republic of Poland of the application for the appointment of the Supreme Court Judge of the candidate submitted by the Minister. It is extremely important that candidates for Supreme Court judges are not evaluated for their professional qualifications. It's about assessing their professional qualifications. The Minister didn't stay. For he will be obliged to present the results of such an assessment to the National Judicial Council, and within 14 days the National Judicial Council will have no opportunity to collect material allowing any assessment of the achievements of the candidates for the most important court in the State, let alone to examine them properly. In addition, the solution proposed in Article. 92 § 4 of the proposal undermines the Constitutional position of the National Judicial Council, bringing it de facto into the role of the opinion-holder of the Minister of Justice's request to appoint a Supreme Court judge. Meanwhile, until art. 179 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland will not be amended by the procedure laid down in Article. 235 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, as long as only the National Council of the Court of Justice may present a request for the appointment of a Supreme Court judge to the President of the Republic of Poland and it is not permissible to introduce by law any presumptions and legal fiction which in practice deprive the Council of the possibility to exercise its constitutional powers only. The influence of the Minister of Justice on the staffing of the Supreme Court will also be exercised by the right to delegate universal courts, including judges who have no experience in appeal proceedings, to act as Supreme Court Judges. The draft allows a Judge by delegation to issue judgments in the Supreme Court in a single seat, and even to have a majority of his members in a three-member composition as Delegated Judges who will thus be able to vote on a Supreme Court Judge who has been properly appointed to the Supreme Court. It should be recalled at this point that it is already permitted to delegate universal courts and military courts to the Supreme Court, but in no case can it constitute the majority of the formation of the court. This in turn means that, after the entry into force of the bill, the Minister of Justice will gain the following extraordinary powers: among the current judges of the Supreme Court, he will indicate those who will remain in the Supreme Court and will be able to rule, appoint a judge who will be the first President of the Supreme Court for many months, who will be able to delegate to the Supreme Court the judges of the Supreme Court, who will be able to vote three judges in the Supreme Court, which, moreover, is not acceptable in the general courts, will only be able to submit a candidate for the Supreme Court to the National Judicial Council in the first election of the Supreme Court judges, who will be the only candidate for the position of Supreme Court President in charge of the work of chambers. In principle, the powers of the Minister of Justice exercised before the Supreme Court judges confer on that politician absolute power over the Supreme Court during the transitional period. In fact, there has already been such a graphic statement that if this project is carried out, the Supreme Court will become the department of the Ministry of Justice. And there's a lot of evidence that this could actually be the case. The powers of the Minister of Justice thus constructed grant this - again I will repeat - to the politician in principle absolute power over the Supreme Court during the transitional period. In the assessment of the National Judicial Council, the content of the transitional provisions of the project refers to some historical solutions which undermine the independence of the judiciary, such as those introduced by the Court of Justice. under the Decree of 8 March 1945 on the partial amendment of the Law on the System of Universal Courts. In addition, the opinion suggests that the current staff of the Supreme Court will cease to be employed, including experts working at the Bureau of Studies and Analysis of the Supreme Court, as well as a drastic reduction in their remuneration. Such regulation violates the principle of citizens' trust in the State, its organs and exposes the Supreme Court to the loss of highly qualified and experienced workers. Those who do not work in this manner will de facto be deprived of their right to court, which will put the State Treasury at risk of the European Court of Human Rights having to pay compensation for this. Now the comments relating to the organizational structure of the Supreme Court judges. As has already been pointed out, the bill in question envisages changes in the organizational structure of the Supreme Court by changing the names of the courts and the establishment of a Disciplinary Chamber. However, the proposal does not fully define the jurisdiction of the Chamber of Public Law or the Chamber of Private Law, leaving these extremely important issues to be determined in the Rules of Procedure. To date, the Supreme Court's Rules of Procedure have been adopted by the General Assembly of Judges of the Supreme Court, which was a proof of the legislator's respect for the independence and distinctiveness of the Supreme Court. The drafters now propose that the Supreme Court rules be issued by the Minister of Justice by means of a regulation. The Rules of Procedure of the Supreme Court issued by the Minister of Justice are also intended to determine the total number of judges in the Supreme Court and its respective chambers. So far, the number of judges of the Supreme Court has been determined by the President of the Republic of Poland. It is assumed that the authorities of the Supreme Court are not involved in this procedure, even in opinion. The rules of operation and the size of the Supreme Court will therefore be defined in an unfettered manner by the Minister of Justice, who, as Prosecutor General, is a party or superior of the parties in most proceedings pending before the Supreme Court. In the opinion of the National Judicial Council, if the drafter wishes, as he claims in the explanatory memorandum, that the Rules of the Supreme Court should be an act of ordinary law, it would be optimal to grant the power to issue a regulation to the President of the Republic of Poland at the request of the General Assembly of Judges of the Supreme Court. The author of the bill is to grant the Disciplinary Chamber of the Supreme Court broad autonomy within the structure of the Supreme Court and entrusts the President of the Supreme Court with special powers, which in principle make him the head of the First Chief Executive of the Supreme Court. That's what Art says. 13 § 7 of this project. The National Judicial Board recalls that the internal structure of the Supreme Court must not lead to the differentiation of the service relations of the Supreme Court judges. The proposed special status of the Disciplinary Board indicates that it will essentially become the second Supreme Court and not part of the Supreme Court. In turn, the award of a higher remuneration to the judges of this Chamber than to all judges and the right to hear cases concerning the Supreme Court indicates that the Disciplinary Chamber is to be a superior court before the Supreme Court. These solutions are inconsistent and in a clear way violate the constitutional standard of regulation of the internal organisation of the Supreme Court and undermine the special status of the first President of the Supreme Court. Here we might add that the draft assumes that the judges in the Disciplinary Chamber of the Supreme Court will have a 40% higher remuneration. So the question is: Why should they be additionally paid in this way? Here we must refer precisely to our constitutional tradition and emphasize that, after the changes in the system in 1989, such rules were adopted that no form of awards or award of judges can be used in the judiciary. Because the idea was not to give an instrument to anyone outside the judicial authority to win the favor of judges. So the question is: Why was such a mechanism introduced? Here I will add that this project assumes that the Supreme Court judges will be able to refer only to this Disciplinary Chamber in matters of employment, which really means that the courts are closed down by way of access to the labour court. There are still procedural rules here which indicate that these disciplinary matters or in the Disciplinary Chamber are to be generally recognised by one person. So this is also a major change, in my view, to the disadvantage of comparing it with the regulation currently in force, where, as we know, at first instance the disciplinary cases were identified by three judges of appeal courts and three judges of the Supreme Court in appeal proceedings. Thus, as regards guarantees of impartiality, this is an example of the fact that these guarantees will be reduced rather than increased. The draft assessed shall be amended as regards the requirements for candidates to be judges of the Supreme Court and the procedure for the recognition of candidates. Until now, the Supreme Court may be judged by a person who holds at least 10 years' experience as a judge, prosecutor, President of the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland, her vice-president or counsel and pursue in Poland the profession of lawyer, legal adviser or notary or is a professor or doctor in law. In practice, in addition to the professors, the rights to the Supreme Court were first and foremost appointed judges of appeals with many years of judicial experience. Proposed solutions, in particular Article. 24 § 1 point 6 of the draft, are characterised by far-reaching casuistics, which justifies the suspicion that they were prepared for specific persons to become judges of the Supreme Court, including in exceptional proceedings, those governed by Article. 92 projects. For example, a Supreme Court judge will be able to become a person whose sole professional experience consists of a five-year job as a prosecutor of the Institute of National Remembrance, or a person who has been appointed as prosecutor of the National Public Prosecutor's Office and who has served for 1 day prior to filing for the vacant post of a judge this office, i.e. practically without any experience. In the opinion of the National Judicial Council, the proposed wording of the Article. 24 § 1 point 6 of the draft law contradicts the existing rules of appointment to the Supreme Court of persons with particularly outstanding substantive qualifications. Moreover, the procedure for examining and evaluating candidates for Supreme Court judges does not provide for a procedure for assessing the qualifications of candidates. At present, each candidate for the Supreme Court's professional and life acquis is subject to a thorough, thorough analysis by the Visitor of his assessment of the candidate's qualifications. However, the projector does not want candidates to be subject to a substantive assessment at the highest court in the State. In addition, it refuses the right to express its opinion on the candidate to the first President of the Supreme Court and to the authorities of the judiciary. The National Judicial Board will only be able to assess the candidate's application on the basis of the content of the application card completed by the applicant himself. Contrary to the suggestions made in the explanatory memorandum of the opinion, it is impossible to define this procedure as transparent, since the National Council of Judicial Affairs and the President of the Republic of Poland will be deprived of the opportunity to familiarize themselves with the professional qualifications of the candidate, his professional achievements and life experience. And here I will add from myself that according to all international standards - it is stressed - the nomination procedure of all judges must be transparent, that there can be no doubt as to why a particular person has been appointed as a judge. If the project does not provide for any criteria that will be assessed and will form the basis for the assessment of the Minister that this is a good candidate for a Supreme Court judge, then it is indeed a fundamental question: How will the Minister know that this is a good candidate? Has the Minister already carried out such an analysis today, e.g. on the potential candidates and on the basis of what will they do? What will the President do if he thinks he's a good candidate for judgeship? So far it has a powerful material gathered in this selection procedure, it has a resolution of the National Courts Council with justification. And what will this justify the National Judicial Council's presentation of this candidate? A number of provisions of the proposed law propose that the Minister of Justice be given significant powers affecting the possibility of taking up or continuing to serve as a judge of the Supreme Court. In particular, the Minister of Justice will decide on matters such as: the possibility for a judge who is approaching 65 years of age and is able, due to the state of health, the possibility for a Supreme Court judge to conduct teaching classes in higher education in more than 50 teaching hours per year, the possibility for a Supreme Court judge to take additional employment in a scientific and teaching position, as well as another occupation or way of earning. This means that the Minister of Justice will have a greater impact on the status of Supreme Court Judge than on the status of Regional Court Judge. In the latter case, the majority of powers are exercised by the competent President of the court, so let us add, someone who is within the judicial authority, while here all powers are granted to the Minister of Justice, who is outside the sphere of judicial authority. [[Voice from the room: There's a change.]] In the latter case, we have the powers of the President of the competent court. The project thus marginalises the role of the first President of the Supreme Court and the President of the Supreme Court, which directs the work of a particular Chamber in which the judge is adjudicated. After the proposed changes have entered into force, the position of the President of the District Court will be stronger and more stable than that of the first President of the Supreme Court and the Presidents of the Supreme Court. With regard to the judicial procedure, the draft under review assumes that the allocation of cases and the appointment of the formation of the Supreme Court judge will belong to the competent President of the Supreme Court, the President of the Chamber. Meanwhile, the Minister of Justice announced that judicial reform would be based, among others. the introduction of a system of random allocation of cases to judges. On the Ministry's website, it can still be read that the random assignment of cases is intended to protect against fraud and to ensure that the case is not assigned to a judge who is deliberately elected to a particular case. For this reason, in the assessment of the National Judicial Council, it is necessary, in the light of the other regulations contained in the draft opinion, to introduce in the Supreme Court the principle of drawing lots of rapporteurs' judges and formations. The new procedure for the appointment of the Presidents of the Supreme Court indicates that the concerns raised by the Minister of Justice regarding the possibility of abuse in the appointment of formations will be justified this time. And now I'd like to say what it looks like in practice right now. Each case in the Supreme Court is assigned in a de facto random manner, because according to the order of influence. The President of the Supreme Court, in charge of the work of the Chamber, shall designate a specific composition and shall assign cases to that composition in the order of influence. So we are dealing de facto with some kind of solution, random award of cases, and this project assumes completely manual control, because each case will be assigned to a specific judge personally by the President in charge of the work of a particular Chamber. The disciplinary responsibility of the judges. The draft law introduces new arrangements for disciplinary proceedings against Supreme Court judges, general court judges and military court judges. The role of the Minister of Justice, which is not only an executive authority, will be significantly strengthened but also takes up the position of the Prosecutor-General and is therefore the superior of all prosecutors in judicial proceedings as parties. The Minister of Justice shall have the power to appoint an ad hoc spokesperson to any case of the Minister of Justice who will be appointed from among the prosecutors presented by the national prosecutor. The Disciplinary Ombudsman of the Minister of Justice shall be entitled to initiate disciplinary proceedings against the Judge at the request of the Minister of Justice. He will also be able to proceed with the ongoing proceedings. Its appointment - that is very important - by the Minister of Justice will be equivalent to excluding from taking action by the competent disciplinary officer of the Supreme Court, the disciplinary officer of the general courts and his alternates and the disciplinary officer of the military courts and his alternates, who are appointed from among the judges for a four-year term. The Disciplinary Ombudsman of the Minister of Justice will be entitled to charge the judges with disciplinary charges and to bring proceedings before a disciplinary tribunal. The law does not exclude persons who may act as disciplinary officer of the Minister of Justice, so the prosecutor will be able to act as prosecutor in a disciplinary court before the judge and as a party or participant in proceedings in a case which such a judge considers to be guilty of, which will be difficult to reconcile with the objective of impartiality of the judge. Thus, it follows that the Minister of Justice may not yet have special confidence in these permanent spokespersons, and the provision expressly allows him that if he has an extra case, which he will be particularly interested in, he will appoint such a disciplinary officer solely for this matter and his appointment will exclude the possibility of acting on those who are normally disciplinary spokespersons. The Minister for Justice of the Prosecutor General, in addition to the power to appoint a Disciplinary Advocate of the Minister of Justice, shall also be entitled to act with a request for disciplinary action against a judge which may result in the initiation of disciplinary proceedings or the refusal to initiate such proceedings. In the event of an order by a disciplinary officer to refuse disciplinary action or to terminate disciplinary proceedings, the Minister of Justice shall be able to object to that provision. The objection will be binding on the disciplinary officer, and therefore, generally speaking, he will have no choice. In the event of an objection, the disciplinary officer will be required to initiate or continue to conduct a previously decommitted disciplinary action, even though he considers that there is no basis for such proceedings. In addition, the statement of the Minister of Justice in opposition to the further proceedings is to be binding on the disciplinary officer, i.e., in one word, the Ombudsman must do as the Minister of Justice tells him. This means that the Minister of Justice and, at the same time, the Attorney General will be entitled to arbitrary and one-man decision to initiate and conduct any disciplinary action against judges of the Supreme Court, general courts and military courts. The position of the Minister of Justice will be extraordinary and significantly stronger than that of the other parties entitled to appear with a request for disciplinary review by the Ombudsman, which, according to the proposal for judges of the Supreme Court, include the first President of the Supreme Court, the President of the Supreme Court, the College of the Supreme Court and the National Prosecutor, and, with regard to the general courts, the President of the Appeal Court, the President of the District Court, the College of Appeals, the College of the District Court and the National Court of Justice, and with regard to judges of the Military Courts, the National Court of Justice, the Minister of Defence, the Presidents of the competent military courts and the College of the competent Military Court, to which the parties will be required to refuse to initiate disciplinary proceedings or to dismiss them will be entitled to a complaint recognised by the disciplinary court. A complaint shall not be binding on a disciplinary officer unless it is taken into account by a disciplinary court. However, if it is taken into account, the Ombudsman shall be bound by the indications of the disciplinary court as to the further course of the proceedings and not, as in the case of the Minister of Justice's opposition, by the indications contained in the appeal lodged, i.e. opposition. So here we have a completely different situation. The proposed legislation should also be strongly negative in so far as it concerns the appointment of judges to rule on disciplinary matters concerning universal courts and military courts at first instance and the appointment of disciplinary representatives in universal and military courts. It is not permissible and contrary to the law that the Minister of Justice, as an executive authority and as a superior of all prosecutors, elects judges to rule in disciplinary courts and entrusts them with these duties. That's what the design art is talking about. 110a § 1 Law on the System of General Courts and Art. 39 § 1 Law on the system of military courts. The Minister of Justice will additionally appoint a disciplinary officer to the courts of the general courts and two Deputy Disciplinary Advocates to the general courts - i.e. a draft Article. 112 § 3 Laws on the system of universal courts - as well as the disciplinary officer of military courts and his deputy - designed Article. 40 § 2 of the Law on the System of Military Courts - who act as prosecutor in disciplinary proceedings and decide whether or not to impose disciplinary charges, which, in certain situations, even if the judge is acquitted before a disciplinary court, may have a significant impact on the further career of the judge. In such a situation, both the prosecutor in disciplinary proceedings and the members of disciplinary courts will be dependent on the representative of the executive authority, which violates not only the constitutional rule of the democratic rule of law, the principle of three divisions of power, but also the right to a court according to which everyone has the right to hear his case by an independent, independent and impartial court. This also applies to judges who are to respond in disciplinary proceedings, because judges are also citizens - I would like to recall that. Moreover, given the fact that it is the Minister of Justice who will decide on the number of judges included in the various disciplinary courts, while the judgment in the disciplinary court will be mandatory for the judge and independent of the performance of his other duties, this term of office of Judge of the disciplinary court will last six years. There is a fear that the delegation of responsibilities to disciplinary courts may be a means of repression in the hands of the Minister of Justice against disobedient judges. The designer shall not introduce any solutions which would allow judges to combine their judicial obligations in a court in which they normally work with the duties of a disciplinary judge, which may be far removed from each other. This may lead to an excessive burden on the work of judges and consequently adversely affect the level of their case law. The proposed solution contradicts the principles of a law which, in the view of the projector, is intended to improve justice and can in fact lead to a long-term process. A further solution which violates the fundamental principles of legal order set out in the Constitution concerns the granting of the Minister of Justice the right to review the disciplinary action of the Court of First Instance and the right of the Minister of Justice to draw attention to the deficiencies found, the request for clarification and the deletion of the consequences of the infringement, i.e. the right to intervene in legal disciplinary proceedings. That's what the design art is talking about. 112d Law on the System of General Courts and Art. 41 Law on the system of military courts. In a democratic legal state, it is unacceptable for an executive authority to interfere with judicial proceedings, and even more so to make any recommendations or decisions binding on the court. Although the draft law regulates that the acts of the Minister of Justice must not enter an area in which members of disciplinary courts are not bound, this regulation is merely of an apparent nature, because it is difficult to imagine what recommendations given to the court by the Minister of Justice are either entering or not entering the sphere of independence of the judge. In the event of failure by a judge who is a member of a disciplinary tribunal, it cannot therefore be ruled out that the Minister of Justice will exercise the powers conferred on him and will appoint a disciplinary officer of the Minister of Justice to initiate disciplinary proceedings against such a judge conducting disciplinary proceedings. These solutions not only violate, but also undermine the principle of a threefold division of power, crossing in an unacceptable way the boundaries of balancing and pervading the executive and judicial powers of democratic states. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 173 of the Constitution, the legislator has placed a particular emphasis on the separation and independence of the judicial authority from the legislative and executive authority, not because it would favour judicial authority in comparison with other authorities, but because it is only in this way that citizens can be protected by their constitutional rights and freedoms, including those expressed in Article. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Constitution the right to a fair examination of their case by an independent, impartial and independent court. A judge who will be dependent on the executive authority, specifically the Minister of Justice, or will be afraid of possible disciplinary consequences in the event of judgment in a different way than the Minister of Justice would have wished, will not provide an adequate guarantee of the protection of the individual's freedoms and constitutional rights. This could undermine citizens' confidence in justice. It should be pointed out that the Constitutional Tribunal, in its judgment of 15 October 2015 in the case of the case of letters of origin. Act Kp 1/15 stated that the granting of competence to the Minister of Justice to request a file of court cases completed by a final judgment and a file of pending cases interferes with the essence of the judicial authority and infringes the principle of judicial independence. The consequence of the provision is also the need to process information containing personal data contained in court proceedings, including sensitive data, which violates the constitutional right of individuals to privacy. So here I would like to point out that the Constitutional Tribunal has said that even the request by the Minister of Justice for an act in the course of proceedings so that the Minister can have a look at these files, violates the principle of independence of the judicial authority, let alone give instructions to the judge as to how to conduct disciplinary proceedings. [[Applause]] The Council takes a negative view of the possibility of the President of the Supreme Court directing the Disciplinar Chamber to the Supreme Court's disciplinary officer asking for explanatory action against the Supreme Court judges who may end up bringing disciplinary actions against the judge and the right to lodge a complaint against the order to refuse or dismiss disciplinary action. Taking into account the fact that the President of the Supreme Court in charge of the Disciplinar Chamber will be responsible for the organisation and work of the Disciplinary Chamber, whose competence is to rule on disciplinary matters of the Supreme Court at first and second instance, it must be stated that the power in question is actually similar - and the same in effect - to the conduct of disciplinary matters by the disciplinary court of its own motion. In fact, the proposed solution seems to be that the Disciplinary Chamber, which is to say that the judges who have been subject to the President of the Chamber, should be competent to hear the complaint brought by that President on the order of the Disciplinary Ombudsman to refuse to initiate or terminate disciplinary proceedings. This is contrary to the fundamental principles of judicial proceedings, since it deprives the judge concerned of the possibility of explaining whether or not to defend his rights and procedural guarantees, [[Applause]] in particular the right to an independent and independent court. It seems quite obvious, because if he is subject to this President and he complains about the decision to refuse disciplinary prosecution of that Judge, then what is the situation in which he is? The President of the Chamber will be able to decide directly or by means of a serviceable judge in charge of the Department of the First Disciplinary Chamber of the Supreme Court, e.g. on the composition of the court of recognition declared by him by his complaint. The rejection of the complaint would involve the appearance by the members of the formation of the court against their superior, which in turn could involve creating conflicts in the Disciplinary Chamber. Even assuming that, in the assessment of the composition of the panel ruling, the complaint of the President of the Chamber will be justified, the social acceptance of the decision will still be negative due to the existing professional dependency. According to Art. 52 of the proposed law on the Supreme Court will reduce the number of judicial formations in disciplinary matters conducted by the Supreme Court judges. In the first instance, these cases will generally be dealt with on a one-man basis and in the second instance on a three-member basis. Currently, in the first instance, there are three- and seven-member warehouses in the second instance. The proposed judicial formations are inadequate to the rank and importance of the disciplinary decision and increase the risk of pressure from the executive authority, in particular when account is taken of the Minister of Justice's numerous powers regarding disciplinary proceedings. At the same time, the drafter shall provide that disciplinary matters of general courts and military courts shall be dealt with in first instance by disciplinary courts in appeal courts or by disciplinary courts in military district courts in three-member formations respectively. The designer has not put forward any arguments which would justify such duality of solutions as regards the composition of disciplinary courts which examine similar cases for general court judges and Supreme Court judges. The proposed solution is not justified, in particular when account is taken of the statutory powers of the Supreme Court, in the same rank and position as its judges. The legislator did not explain in the draft why, in his judgment, the disciplinary matters of general courts and military courts require an in-depth factual and legal analysis of those disciplinary proceedings which will concern the judges of the Supreme Court, since these cases are identical and based on the same grounds of disciplinary responsibility. The proposal to publish disciplinary judgments on the Supreme Court's website, as discussed in the draft article, is negative. 109a Law on the system of common courts, publication of the judgment constitutes an additional sanction not provided for in the catalogue of disciplinary penalties. This leads to double punishment for disciplinary misconduct, so conviction for a specific disciplinary penalty for a delict, and also for the publication of this judgment. Moreover, the publication of disciplinary decisions in any case, including in the event of a judge being punished with a penalty of warning for minor disciplinary offences, is not justified by the public interest and may entail a weakening of public confidence in such a judge and may result in, for example, later. a large number of requests to exclude it from judgment, which would normally be recognised by that judge, which would in fact lead to an extension of the duration of judicial proceedings and would have a negative impact on the functioning of the courts. In addition, the publication of disciplinary decisions in all cases could reduce citizens' confidence in justice. I will add here, aside from perhaps a further part of the justification for this passage, that now all disciplinary decisions of the Supreme Court are of course available because they are published and disciplinary action is fully public, i.e. disciplinary proceedings shall be conducted in accordance with the rules of criminal procedure and any citizen interested in the outcome of such proceedings may come to the court and look at these proceedings, both before the court of first instance and before the court of second instance. There is no secret here as to disciplinary action, there is only a problem of this kind, whether the introduction of the obligation to publish these judgments is not really an additional penalty, a form of additional penalty. The proposalor also introduces regulations which significantly restrict the procedural rights of the accused in the course of disciplinary proceedings. According to the draft article. 113 § 2 The Law on the System of General Courts at the reasoned request of a convicted judge who cannot take part in disciplinary proceedings on grounds of illness, the President of the Disciplinary Court or the Disciplinary Court shall appoint a defender for him from among lawyers or lawyers. However, in the light of the proposed Article. 113a The Law on the System of General Courts is a right of defence which is apparent because that provision provides that actions relating to the appointment of a defender and the taking of a defence by him do not hinder the conduct of the proceedings. Thus, it cannot be ruled out that until a public defender or defender is appointed, which involves, among other things, the appointment of a public defender. the need to examine the case files and to establish an appropriate line of defence with the accused judge, as well as the gathering and filing of evidence, the case will be terminated or other relevant procedural proceedings will be carried out. In fact, the accused judge will be deprived of the constitutional right to court, which also consists of the right of defence. Since the legislator introduces mandatory defence in certain situations, it should also introduce procedural safeguards to ensure that such defence is realistic and guarantees the minimum procedural standards in democratic countries that are blamed. A further provision restricting the constitutional right to the court of the accused judge is a draft article. 121 § 3 Laws on the system of universal courts according to which Article 3 shall not apply in appeal proceedings. 454 Code of Criminal Procedure. Article 4 is replaced by the following: 454 § 1 of the Code of Criminal Procedure provides that the court of appeal may not convict the defendant who was acquitted at first instance or for whom the proceedings were dismissed or conditionally dismissed at first instance. This provision deprives the accused judge of the right to a two-instant disciplinary action because, in the event of a court of first instance acquittal and subsequent conviction by the court of second instance, the accused judge will be deprived of the possibility of appeal against a judgment declaring him guilty and punishable by disciplinary action, even if such a judgment is defective. The provision in question is incompatible with Article. 176 the Constitution, which provides that judicial proceedings are at least two-way. It is generally accepted in the doctrine and in the Judaism that the constitutional principle of the duality of judicial proceedings requires, in particular, access to the Court of First Instance and, consequently, the granting of appropriate remedies to the party. And here, too, we have many statements from the representatives of the doctrine on this subject. Designed provision of the article. 115c The Law on the System of General Courts refers to disciplinary proceedings which allow the use of evidence obtained in breach of the law against the accused judge and as a result of the application of operational control. However, the proposer does not see the purpose of disciplinary action, which is not the same as the purpose of criminal proceedings. The doctrine assumes that disciplinary responsibility primarily serves to protect the image of the judicial service in the sense that it indicates the functioning of higher standards for judges, their proper enforcement in the event of their breach. However, this does not mean that disciplinary action is tantamount to criminal proceedings, since it concerns the vast majority of acts that do not exhaust the signs of crime. The proper application of the criminal procedure has a particular warranty value, as it ensures, on the one hand, a certain procedural protection of the rights of the accused judge, on the other hand, gives disciplinary action appropriate to the importance of social delicacies of seriousness. Where a disciplinary measure at the same time exhausts the marks of a criminal offence, disciplinary liability shall be fully independent in the sense that the criminal liability of a judge does not relieve him of professional and disciplinary liability. The National Judicial Council strongly condemns the draft article. 104 a law on the basis of which the Minister of Justice will be able to demand the renewal of any disciplinary action completed by a final judgment given by a disciplinary officer before the date of entry into force of this Law. It must be stressed that disciplinary agents are independent bodies, acting on the basis and within the limits of the law in the light of the laws currently in force. The possibility, in principle, of an unlimited reopening of final proceedings undermines the fundamental principles of legal order, in particular legal certainty. The right of the Minister of Justice to apply for renewal was based on inaccurate, in fact discretionary grounds set out in such a way as the good of justice or the disclosure of new facts or evidence. First of all, the first of these conditions is extremely capacious and can in principle cover any case in which the disciplinary officer has finalized the case. It must be stressed that, in the current legal state, the Minister of Justice has the right to request the reopening of disciplinary proceedings, however, the reopening of proceedings to the detriment of the accused may take place if the remission of proceedings or the judgment has taken place as a result of a criminal offence, or if, within 5 years of the remission or judgment, new circumstances or evidence which may justify the conviction or the execution of a more severe sentence are brought to light. The solution presented in the draft opinion will allow for unlimited renewal of disciplinary proceedings, including proceedings completed many years ago, which, in the light of the statute of limitations, undermines the legitimacy of limitation institutions. The introduction of such solutions also undermines confidence in the authorities of the State and the rule of law. And finally, the conclusions. In the assessment of the National Judicial Council, the entry into force of the provisions in question will be tantamount to the actual suspension of the application of the constitutional principles of the threefold division and balance of the authorities and the principle of the separation and independence of the judiciary. Given that the Supreme Court exercises oversight over the activities of universal courts and military courts and that the right to court is a fundamental means of protecting the rights of the individual, the National Council of the Court of Justice is concerned about the possibility of legal protection for citizens against abuse of public authority after such changes have been introduced. It should be noted here that the explanatory memorandum of the draft is based on examples of Supreme Court rulings which have been negatively assessed by the drafter. This section of the statement of reasons would be a justification for the exchange of judicial staff and the change in the composition of the Supreme Court. I would like to stress that the case law of the Supreme Court should be assessed differently, from a substantive point of view, it should not translate into any personal issues. The author of the justification for this project, which is called the parliamentary project, stated that it is part of a broader reform of the justice system, which is intended to guarantee justice in court judgments, to speed up proceedings and to restore public confidence in the court. It must be said with disappointment that none of the so-called elements presented by the Minister of Justice has been present in the past 20 months. judicial reform does not lead to such objectives. The law on the Supreme Court will not guarantee fair judgments or restore public confidence in the court, nor will it make the courts seen as independent, but rather as a subordinate minister of justice, filled by him and controlled by him. Contrary to the announcements made by the author of the justification for this project, the entry into force of this project will not speed up court proceedings, and in terms of the Supreme Court, it will actually paralyze its activities due to the exchange of judges on a large scale. However, a group of several dozen judges of the Supreme Court or of the employees of the Supreme Court who will, perhaps, be expelled from work, but all citizens and other persons who are under the jurisdiction of the Polish State, any person who is wrongly accused of the slightest crime, all who remain or may become a party to a legal dispute with a politician or a state office, whether they are big traders or ordinary consumers. The Supreme Court, although formally it will, of course, exist, will be deprived of the necessary authority, substantive and adequate qualifications, can be added, social trust. All changes in the Supreme Court must be analysed in the context of the function of this court as a guarantee of the regularity of the parliamentary and presidential elections. The Supreme Court recognises complaints about the activities of the State Election Commission and electoral protests, as well as resolutions on the validity of elections and referendums. In fact, the submission of Supreme Court judges to one politician gives rise to very serious and justified concerns about objectivity and integrity of control, e.g. the legality of elections. It is therefore important to express such deep concern about the way in which this project is recognised. He was brought to the Sejm as a parliamentary project, although some Members admit that they had not read it before signing it. In addition, the first reading of this project was carried out in this way, and I can already update that the National Judicial Council had not been able to give an opinion as provided for in the Act. I would add from myself that it seems quite obvious that this project is unconstitutional in many places and its adoption will lead to a breach of the guarantee of independence of the judiciary and judges. Without these guarantees, judges become irresistible to external attempts to interfere with their settlement. The concept of public service also clearly includes work in the Chancellery of the First President of the Supreme Court and the Office of Studies and Analysis of the Supreme Court. As stated by the Constitutional Tribunal in its judgment of 23 January 2014, e.g. K 51/12, access to the public service covers not only the stage of entering the public service but also the stage of remaining in the public service until leaving it. From the article. 60 of the Constitution follows, according to the Court, an order to lay down clear and clear rules not only for admission to service but also for the same rules applicable to discharge from service. Meanwhile, the designed art. 90. The 2th Act does not contain any criteria for the substantive verification of the previous staff of the Supreme Court. The proposed mechanism boils down to the fact that if the new working conditions and wages are not proposed without a motive to the court's employees, their employment relations will expire by virtue of the law itself. The employer under the proposed provision will therefore be able to decide in a completely arbitrary manner who will continue to maintain his status as a worker. The work of these people - contrary to the guarantees of the article. 24, which says that the work is under the protection of the Republic of Poland - will not be subject to any protection from the Republic of Poland. Art. 90. Nor does the 2nd project provide objective criteria for the selection of persons to continue to perform public service, does not ensure equal opportunities and non-discrimination.
