These rules will be arbitrary and not subject to external scrutiny by the employer, and it is therefore justified to believe that this provision is incompatible with both Article. 24, as well as Article. 60 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. I would also like to refer to the fact that the proposed law provides - and this is mentioned in the justification - for speeding up the work of the Supreme Court. In accordance with the text of the proposed Article. 91 by the date of adoption by the Minister of Justice of a regulation setting out the number of judges in the Supreme Court, the Chamber of Public Law will have 14 judges, the Chamber of Private Law will have 18 judges and the Disciplinary Chamber will have 12 Supreme Court judges. In my view, the determination at such a level - even during a transitional period - of the number of judges of the Supreme Court may affect the constitutional right of the court to consider the case without undue delay, i.e. the standard expressed in Article. 45 constitutions. If we look at a document published by the Supreme Court every year - this year it is information about the activities of the Supreme Court in 2016 - as of 31 December 2016 - the Supreme Court had 83 judges and 11 427 cases were dealt with, including 7859 cassation and cassation complaints, over 1100 complaints and 165 legal issues. It cannot therefore be accepted that if, during this transitional period, the composition of judges is halved, the Supreme Court will without delay recognise the cases which are referred to the Supreme Court. Designed art. 91 therefore undermines the efficiency of proceedings before the Supreme Court. Moreover, this allegation becomes obvious if we look at the proposed Chamber of Private Law to identify cases which so far fall within the jurisdiction of the Civil Chamber, the Criminal Chamber and the Chamber of Labour, Social Insurance and Public Affairs. There were 7886 cassation and cassation complaints lodged in these chambers themselves, whereas only 6 cassations were filed in the Military Chamber, while 14 judicial positions are designed for its counterpart, the Chamber of Public Law. In turn, the Supreme Court in 2016 recognized 152 disciplinary matters concerning the activities of the Supreme Court, and yet the Disciplinary Board is to count 12 judges during the transitional period. In my view, these figures show that designers are not concerned at all with citizens' cases before the Supreme Court being dealt with within a reasonable period of time and without undue delay. If they had, they wouldn't have proposed in the article. 91 of the proposed law on content which is incompatible with Article. 45 constitutions. That's another line. According to art. 92. 2 of the proposed law, the Minister for Justice shall present one candidate to the National Judicial Council for each position of the Supreme Court of the persons who fulfil the conditions laid down in Article. 24. This solution infringes the constitutional principle of separation of powers, the principle of independence and separation of judicial authority, as well as Article. 179 constitutions. If we now look at the concept of government administration management, it does not include the competence of the Minister of Justice to decide who will be presented to the National Judicial Council as a candidate for a Supreme Court judge. Let's go back to what I said at the beginning - the Minister of Justice under the Constitution is a member of the National Judicial Council and that is the scope of its ability to act in relation to the National Judicial Council, but there is no possibility - within the concept of managing the government administration - to decide who will be presented to the National Judicial Council as a candidate for a Supreme Court judge. In this respect, the role of the Minister of Justice is described in detail in the Constitution and it comes down to only one vote within the National Judicial Council when voting on the presentation of a candidate for judge to the President - Article. 179 constitutions. Art. 179 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland only provides for the participation of the KRS as a collegial body in the procedure for the appointment of a judge. The proposed solution therefore aims to subjugate the Supreme Court to the executive authority by entrusting the Minister of Justice with exclusivity in presenting candidates for Supreme Court judges during a transitional period. This is not only in line with the principle of judicial independence, but also in the entire system of protection of human rights. According to art. 93 § The President of the Supreme Court of the Chamber of Public Law, the President of the Supreme Court of the Chamber of Private Law, and the President of the Supreme Court of the Disciplinary Chamber shall for the first time appoint the President at the request of the Minister of Justice from the judges of the Supreme Court in the active state. This provision is also incompatible with the principle of three divisions of power and the principle of independence and separation of judicial authority. It is impossible to speak of the separation and independence of the judicial authority, if only the executive authority decides to fill executive positions in the highest judicial authority. This is the case for the proposed Article. 93. 2 which provides only for the cooperation of the executive authorities in the appointment of the Presidents of the Supreme Court Chambers. The proposed provision is also incompatible with Article. Article 149 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 the Constitution, which gives the Minister the power to direct the government administration but not the courts, including the Supreme Court, by creating the personnel cast of its internal organs. The proposed regulations concerning the functioning of the Supreme Court of the Disciplinary Chamber also raise significant concerns. Firstly, consideration should be given to the reasonableness of setting up this Chamber. If the Supreme Court's information in 2016 shows that 62 disciplinary matters concerning judges and 90 disciplinary cases concerning lawyers, legal advisers, notaries, prosecutors and doctors and pharmacists were dealt with at that time in the Supreme Court, then the question is whether a special separate chamber is really necessary or not. Let's see, the impact of other cases that are going on by the Supreme Court was 11,000. cases, and now for these 152 disciplinary cases, a special Disciplinar Chamber is created. The question is whether this is a waste of public power and resources. It draws attention in the project not only to the fact that the Chamber itself is established, but also to the possibilities it will have. It will be characterised by information autonomy - art. 6, Art. 7 and Article 7 13 draft law, budgetary autonomy - article. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 draft law, executive autonomy, and finally separate rules for the remuneration of judges in this Chamber. It is worth recalling that art. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 7 project provides an additional 40% the basic salary, together with the functional allowance for those judges who decide to work and judge in this Disciplinary Chamber. These solutions undermine the organisational and staff integrity of the Supreme Court. In addition, the proposed article. Article 41 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 7 is incompatible with Article. Point (a) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 2 of the Constitution, which provides that judges are to be remunerated corresponding to the dignity of the office and the scope of their duties. And here I stress the words: the scope of their responsibilities, because if the number of disciplinary matters is grossly less than the number of cases that affect other chambers of the Supreme Court, what can be justified by the fact that these judges should actually be increased by 40% ? If we look at current statistics, the impact of cases will be de facto seventy times less, and the remuneration of each judge ruling will be 40% less. higher. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Some of the shortcomings presented in the project are essential in terms of whether the proposed Supreme Court can be regarded as a separate and independent judicial authority. These defects make it necessary to refuse such qualities to the Supreme Court in its proposed form. Moreover, during the transitional period, the proposed cast of executive positions and judges in the Supreme Court infringes the principle of judicial independence, which is the foundation of the protection of the rights and freedoms of the individual. It causes the Supreme Court to become in practice a body affiliated to the executive authorities. And if we look at all of this not only from an institutional point of view, but also from the point of view of the individual's rights and freedoms, we have to ask whether the citizen who will be in dispute with the state authorities, with the Treasury companies, with the local authorities that may depend on the central authority - if these are matters that may have certain negative consequences for the State Treasury... [[It's not gonna get any worse.]] ... matters relating to the liability of the State Treasury if this court recognizes various types of prosecutorial redemptions - whether this citizen will not be in a losing position from the beginning, since such changes are introduced by this law, but also, of course, by the Act on the National Council of Courts and through the Law on the system of universal courts. In this context, this raises the greatest concern - and this is, I think, also the expression of today's debate - that such changes are being made quickly, hastily, and should be considered not to touch citizens so that they do not violate... [[We had time to think.]] ...that which is the content of the constitution. If, ladies and gentlemen, we can imagine an internist to whom a patient with shortness of breath comes, then this internist isn't gonna do heart surgery right away, but he's gonna be looking for a good cardiac surgeon... [[Eight years.]] ...will be looking for people to advise him on how best to guide it, not performing open-heart surgery that could lead to the death of a patient. And in this context, I would like to stress that what is also worrying me is that the project was not subject to appropriate, deep public consultation. This was said by the chairman of the National Judicial Council, who did not even have time to take a stand on this matter. I am also talking about the fact that there are various restrictions on admission to committee meetings, or the fact that there is no willingness to hold a proper public hearing on the proposed law. And in this context, at the very end, I would like to read the position that has been prepared by the Agreement of Professional Self-Governments and Law Associations. This is the position of today. This is an agreement bringing together all the legal authorities, as well as associations that employ lawyers - assistants, referenda, judges. I would like to read this: The Agreement of Professional Self-Governments and Law Associations expresses strong opposition to both the solutions proposed in the draft law on the Supreme Court and the procedure for proceding the act of cardinal importance for the Polish justice system. The draft law on the Supreme Court remains incompatible with the constitutional model, represents another step in abolishing the three divisions of power and is an expression of a break with the principle of independence of the judiciary. Giving the Minister of Justice further powers - this time with regard to the highest court in the hierarchy of courts in Poland - poses an even greater threat to all democratic rules, that it is the Supreme Court that determines the validity of national electoral acts, including presidential and parliamentary elections. The actual submission of the Supreme Court to politicians through the creation of mechanisms of permanent supervision and extensive powers forming de facto discretionaryly the professional relationship of the judges of this court will certainly not only affect confidence in its decisions, but also runs counter to the fundamental principles of democracy guaranteed by the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The destruction of the existing structure of the Supreme Court must be read only as a pretext for further human action against the functionary judges of the Supreme Court and the judges of the jurors of the existing chambers, while the solutions proposed by the draft law in the area of the Disciplinary Chamber and the powers of the President of this Chamber introduce the unknown structure of the offices and courts of the dio-government. The act of such cardinal importance for the Polish legal order should also be preceded by broad public consultations guaranteeing that all stakeholders can make their views known. The Agreement of Professional Self-Governments and Law Associations calls for action to be stopped leading to the destabilisation of the Polish legal order, not only in constitutional order, but also in the interests of citizens. They signed: Chairman of the agreement legal counsel Maciej Bobrowicz and secretary of agreement Dr Paweł Skuczyński. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On the other hand, I will always protest against using legal comparative arguments, using the experience of other countries and showing that it is different in another country. In each country it is regulated in its own way. This is linked to the legal culture, tradition and various types of issues that affect how the institutions function in a given constitutional reality. And even if in some countries it may seem that the executive authority has far-reaching powers, that does not mean that these powers are abused or abused. And here's one example. It was said that the President of the United States is calling Supreme Court judges. However, first of all, it does not revoke them, and second of all, the entire structure of the Supreme Court is constructed in such a way that the judges remain in office for life unless they give up on their own. And if there is a situation, that the judge is leaving - whether it is. Just as Judge Scalia, who died, has passed away, there is this space for the appointment of a new judge. Presidents change, and sometimes the composition of the Supreme Court is exactly the same, only one judge who becomes so-called. swing vote. [[Exactly.]] This is a completely different situation than here, when the law simply provides for the dismissal of all the judges of the Supreme Court and the appointment of those whom the Minister of Justice will indicate. ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-18-sejm-46-1.u503	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Point (b) of Article 4 (1) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 is replaced by the following: 5, which explicitly states that the constitutional changes relating to the court have the right to be adopted by Parliament by ordinary law, and this is the interpretation of all constitutionalists in this regard. For example. the opinion of the Supreme Bar Council does not give rise to any response to this fundamental problem on which this entire law is based. In short, I want to answer the questions, I will not mention the names of the Members and Members of Parliament. These questions concerned inter alia. whether there are judges who have never ruled before. I cannot answer this question precisely, but from what you said, the first president of the Supreme Court in your speech, 1/3 of the current Supreme Court judges are professors of law. Perhaps someone has become a judge of the Supreme Court before being a judge, but this is, of course, permitted under the provisions of the current law. What kind of cases will go to the insurance room... where the insurance company cases will go? They will go to the Chamber of Private Law, as you know from the article. 3. This will be decided by the order of the Minister of Justice. Will this law change the duration of proceedings? This law, High Izbo, is primarily intended to change the duration of proceedings in the Supreme Court, before all else in the Supreme Court. According to the information on the activities of the Supreme Court submitted by you, the first President of the Supreme Court is waiting in the Civil Chamber for the resolution of a cassation complaint for 12 months, in the Chamber of Labour and Social Security - 9... [[It's not chronic.]] ...in the Penalty Chamber, as well. [[The most efficient court in Poland.]] The seven-month average referred to by the President is dealt with by the Military Chamber, where the proceedings are the fastest within a month, even two months, but only because there are the smallest number of cases and the smallest number of judges in this Chamber - there are only six judges and 88 cases in 2016. Should there be an increase in the participation of citizens - another question - as stated in Article. 182 constitutions. Of course, but citizens take part in the justice system primarily with regard to the courts of first instance. This question does not concern the legal matter that we are discussing today. Is a candidate who has been a judge of the district court, district court, or a court of appeal for 10 years above all the judges of the district court a good candidate for a Supreme Court judge? Surely there are outstanding lawyers, young judges promising well and they could, of course, have the right to such a rapid promotion, but this will be decided by the relevant resolutions of the National Judicial Council. As far as the catalogue of cases to be dealt with by chambers is concerned, there was a question of why it is not regulated by law, only to be governed by the Minister of Justice's regulation. This is due to the legislative economy, that is how it can be said, because every addition of a new type of case should be amended by law, and if the powers of the Supreme Court are increased by ordinary law, then the matter can be dealt with quickly by a regulation of the Minister of Justice. It was also a question of what percentage of cassation complaints are not dealt with. The question is, what percentage is this, I can't answer precisely right now. Anyway, for the so-called. there are relatively many pre-courts, i.e. at the sitting of an secret court composed of one judge, the judge refuses to accept a cassation complaint with a brief justification that there is no problem to be resolved as a legal issue. Therefore, a cassation complaint shall be refused. Why are we shortening the term of the Supreme Court magistrates? We do not shorten the term of the Supreme Court Judges, because the term of office does not exist in the case of Supreme Court Judges except for the first Supreme Court President - here is a 6-year term, which follows from the article. Point (c) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 3 Constitutions. There was also talk of legislative lawlessness, about the fact that we are clearing the court from communism. We didn't say anything like that, nor was it in the justification that we were doing some sort of cleansing from the Communists. The president herself said in her speech that this matter, as she said, had already been dealt with in biology. This is not the case for the revision of these system rules and the rules on the functioning of the Supreme Court. First of all, the changes are profound with regard to the organisation of chambers: the Chamber of Public Law, the Chamber of Private Law and the Disciplinar Chamber, which raises such concerns from the opposition. As a lawyer with 40 years of experience, I deeply believe in judicial independence. In my line of work, I've always followed that rule. I've never been guided by any pressure on judges. If I disagreed with their decisions, then there were appeals, of course they were different names, but I was so taught in court applications that if there is a case, there is no comment on court decisions. That's what appeals are for, that's what second instance is for, that's what extraordinary remedies are for. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, pursuant to Article. 51 point 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland, I apply for the second reading of the draft law on the Supreme Court immediately after the first reading, without referring the draft law to the committee. [[Especially by the president.]] ...the most important of the courts there is no reform of the Polish justice system. [[Applause]] Without reform in disciplinary proceedings, there is no reform of the Polish judiciary. This project refers to the pre-war model of the Supreme Court, the system model of the elite Supreme Court, which is to ensure the uniformity of case law, which is to care for the highest moral and ethical standards. This project restores public confidence in justice. This project restores trust in and control of justice. [[What are you talking about? Control of the justice system?]] This project breaks up with corporateism, with palism, with clicks and with coopting. The co-optation that has been mentioned so many times in recent times seems to be the way that the Supreme Court judges decide who will be the Supreme Court judge. Colleagues decide on a colleague, in short. There is no external factor that would determine who would be the Supreme Court judge. Therefore, by moving on to concrete solutions, I would like to point out a major change in this area. The General Assembly of the Supreme Court will no longer decide on the candidates for Supreme Court judges. The National Judicial Council is decisive in this respect and in accordance with Article. 25 § 3 the application shall be submitted to the National Judicial Board. A model of coopulation, a corporate model in which the Supreme Court decides who will be a member of it, is an asset on a European scale. I would like to point out here the German system, where the Ministers of Justice of 16 Lands together with the Bundestag decide who will judge the Supreme Court. There is no solution in our system in any European country. In Belgium, the Supreme Court is set up in such a way that the king chooses the judges of the Supreme Court from a list drawn up by the House of Representatives or the Senate. In Denmark, the Minister for Justice, as indicated by the judicial council appointed by the Minister for Justice, shall decide on the appointments to the Supreme Court. In Spain, the appointment is made by a royal regulation at the request of the Supreme Council of the judiciary, together with a countersignation of whom? Minister of Justice. In Ireland, the President, at the request of the government, in Lithuania, the Sejm, from the nomination of the President, in Luxembourg, the Grand Duke of Luxembourg. In Norway, the Supreme Court judges appoint who? Government. At Slovenia - State Assembly at the request of the judicial council, in Switzerland - Federal Assembly. The last country of this type is Sweden, where the Minister of Justice does so at the request of the Council for Justice. The appointments of the magistrates. There is no country that would be organized as it is in our system in which corporateism and co-opting are rules that govern the appointment to the Supreme Court. It gives birth to pathology, it gives birth to cliques, it gives birth to cohabitation. This corporateism causes us to deal with situations in which there are such disgraceful judgments as Minister Zbigniew Ziobro said today. I would just like to refer to this and point out two examples of the decisions of the Disciplinary Chamber of the Supreme Court. The Supreme Court, after a 10-year case involving a judge accused and convicted by a criminal court for forgery of documents, finds that he will not be punished with a disciplinary penalty for removal from the profession, although this would be appropriate to the extent of the blame, as this act has become blurred in the social memory and would therefore be detrimental to the excessive, inadequate situation of severity. Another such case concerned a judge who lied when photographed by a photocopier at the crime scene. You might say, great unpunished crimes cause impunity for small wines. In these small cases there is a particularly visible cover-up of the affairs of colleagues, there is a noticeable this cognition, indulgence and corporateism that we are dealing with. The Supreme Court, of course, acquitted this judge, said that he had the right to lie. The Supreme Court, who decided about the disciplinary delusion of the judge's lies, states that yes, he had the right to lie. These are the standards of the Supreme Court. Therefore, without reform of the most important judicial authority, we will not reform the Polish justice system. The fish breaks from the head. The project is important, necessary, I ask the High House to support it. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-07-18-sejm-46-1.u827	Mr. Marshal! Prime Minister! High Izbo! I wanted to ask two substantive questions about this project. First question. As indicated in the explanatory memorandum of the project, the Minister of Justice will issue a regulation determining the number of judges in a disciplinary court. The authors of the project also point to the need to issue a regulation in relation to a number of amendments both in the Supreme Court Act and in other laws amended by the proposed project. The Minister of Justice shall, by means of a regulation, determine the number of judges in each disciplinary court at the appeal court, on the basis of organisational considerations and the need to ensure the smooth conduct of disciplinary matters. What substantive arguments did they consider necessary to deprive the judicial authority or the President of the court concerned of the power to determine the size of the disciplinary court? What justifies the violation of the principle of judicial independence also at the level of the personal composition of the disciplinary judiciary, which is one of the main elements of the amendment? Second question. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: 20 and Article 20 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers (OJ L 347, 20.12.2013, p. 22 of the draft rules lay down the powers of the Assembly of Judges of the Chamber of the Supreme Court and of the College of the Supreme Court to be limited in relation to the current powers of the said bodies. First of all, the assembly of judges of the Chamber of Supreme Court shall be deprived of the power of opinion of candidates for the position of Supreme Court Judge, while the College shall lose its responsibility for determining the division of activities in the Supreme Court, taking decisions concerning the internal organisational structure of the Supreme Court, opinion of candidates for the President of the Supreme Court, adoption of the Rules of Procedure for their drawing up, opinion of the Rules of Procedure for the election of candidates for the position of Supreme Court Judge, the First President of the Supreme Court and members of the National Court of Justice and the Rules of the Supreme Court. In view of the above, was it the intention of the authors of the project to regulate the role of the internal organs of the Supreme Court so that their significantly limited competence would lead to the dependence of the Supreme Court on the executive authority? I would like to conclude by saying that there are citizens in front of Parliament. Citizens who look at the power of the hands who look at the hands of Members. Citizens who, in their white-red heart, have a desire for Poland to be a democratic country where citizens' rights are respected. I appeal to the PiS Members: show that you are real patriots, act for the sake of your country, withdraw this anti-democratic project from the Sejm.
